class Solution:
    def sortString(self, s: str) -> str:
        freq = {}
        letters = sorted(set(s))
        res = ""
        for i in s:
            if i in freq:
                freq[i]+=1
            else:
                freq[i] = 1
        while freq:
            for i in letters:
                if i in freq:
                    if freq[i]>0:
                        res+=i
                        freq[i]-=1
                    else:
                        del freq[i]
                        self.undefinedMethod()

                if i  in freq:
                    if freq[i]>0:
                        res+=i
                        freq[i]-=1
                        
                        del freq[i]
                        self.undefinedMethod()


        return res

class Solution:
    def sortString(self, s: str) -> str:
        freq = {}
        letters = sorted(set(s))
        res = ""

                freq[i]+=1

                freq[i] = 1

                if i in freq:

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;
- moving from a local repair into a fresh branch of speculative causes when the current path has not been rechecked yet;
- turning a focused fix into a broad rewrite unless the surrounding code clearly forces it.

class Solution:
    def sortString(self, s: str) -> str:
        freq = {}
        for ch in s:
            freq[ch] = freq.get(ch, 0) + 1
        letters = sorted(freq.keys())
        res = []
        while freq:
            for ch in letters:
                if ch in freq:
                    res.append(ch)
                    freq[ch] -= 1
                    if freq[ch] == 0:
                        del freq[ch]
            for ch in reversed(letters):
                if ch in freq:
                    res.append(ch)
                    freq[ch] -= 1
                    if freq[ch] == 0:
                        del freq[ch]
        return ''.join(res)

class Solution:
    def sortString(self, s: str) -> str:
        freq = {}
        for ch in s:
            freq[ch] = freq.get(ch, 0) + 1

        letters = sorted(freq)
        res = []

        while freq:
            for ch in letters:
                if ch in freq:
                    res.append(ch)
                    freq[ch] -= 1
                    if freq[ch] == 0:
                        del freq[ch]

            for ch in reversed(letters):
                if ch in freq:
                    res.append(ch)
                    freq[ch] -= 1
                    if freq[ch] == 0:
                        del freq[ch]

        return ''.join(res)

class Solution:
    def sortString(self, s: str) -> str:
        freq = {}
        for ch in s:
            freq[ch] = freq.get(ch, 0) + 1

        letters = sorted(freq)
        res = []

        while freq:
            for ch in letters:
                if ch in freq:
                    res.append(ch)
                    freq[ch] -= 1
                    if freq[ch] == 0:
                        del freq[ch]

            for ch in reversed(letters):
                if ch in freq:
                    res.append(ch)
                    freq[ch] -= 1
                    if freq[ch] == 0:
                        del freq[ch]

        return ''.join(res)

class Solution:
    def sortString(self, s: str) -> str:
        freq = {}
        letters = sorted(set(s))
        res = ""
        for i in s:
            if i in freq:
                freq[i] += 1
            else:
                freq[i] = 1
        while freq:
            for i in letters:
                if i in freq:
                    if freq[i] > 0:
                        res += i
                        freq[i] -= 1
                        if freq[i] == 0:
                            del freq[i]
            for i in letters[::-1]:
                if i in freq:
                    if freq[i] > 0:
                        res += i
                        freq[i] -= 1
                        if freq[i] == 0:
                            del freq[i]
        return res

This is closer to original. But note: In forward loop, if `i in freq` and `freq[i] > 0`, we decrement and delete if zero. If `freq[i]` is 0, we would not enter, but we never leave zero keys. Let's examine original code more closely:

```
while freq:
    for i in letters:
        if i in freq:
            if freq[i]>0:
                res+=i
                freq[i]-=1
            else:
                del freq[i]
                self.undefinedMethod()

```

The forward loop's `else` branch is intended to delete zero-count keys. But because it doesn't delete after decrement to zero, zero keys remain until next while iteration. If we remove undefined and keep else, it would eventually delete zero keys. But reverse loop's unconditional del is definitely wrong. A minimal fix could be:

```
while freq:
    for i in letters:
        if i in freq:
            if freq[i] > 0:
                res += i
                freq[i] -= 1
            if freq[i] == 0:
                del freq[i]
    for i in letters[::-1]:
        if i in freq:
            if freq[i] > 0:
                res += i
                freq[i] -= 1
            if freq[i] == 0:
                del freq[i]
```

This removes undefined and deletes after decrement. But note in forward loop, after decrement, if freq[i] == 0, delete. If freq[i] was 0 (shouldn't happen), delete. In reverse loop, same. This is minimal and correct. It also avoids the `else` branch. This is perhaps the clearest fix. It uses `res += i` string concatenation. class Solution:
    def sortString(self, s: str) -> str:
        freq = {}
        letters = sorted(set(s))
        res = ""
        for i in s:
            if i in freq:
                freq[i] += 1
            else:
                freq[i] = 1

        while freq:
            for i in letters:
                if i in freq:
                    if freq[i] > 0:
                        res += i
                        freq[i] -= 1
                    if freq[i] == 0:
                        del freq[i]

            for i in letters[::-1]:
                if i in freq:
                    if freq[i] > 0:
                        res += i
                        freq[i] -= 1
                    if freq[i] == 0:
                        del freq[i]

        return res

Need check indentation: `if freq[i] == 0: del freq[i]` is inside `if i in freq:` but outside `if freq[i] > 0:`. So if `freq[i]` is 0 (shouldn't happen), it deletes. If `freq[i]` >0, decrement then if zero delete. Good. If `freq[i]` >0 and after decrement >0, no delete. Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
class Solution:
    def sortString(self, s: str) -> str:
        freq = {}
        letters = sorted(set(s))
        res = ""

        for ch in s:
            if ch in freq:
                freq[ch] += 1
            else:
                freq[ch] = 1

        while freq:
            for ch in letters:
                if ch in freq:
                    if freq[ch] > 0:
                        res += ch
                        freq[ch] -= 1
                    if freq[ch] == 0:
                        del freq[ch]

            for ch in letters[::-1]:
                if ch in freq:
                    if freq[ch] > 0:
                        res += ch
                        freq[ch] -= 1
                    if freq[ch] == 0:
                        del freq[ch]

        return res
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
class Solution:
    def sortString(self, s: str) -> str:
        freq = {}
        letters = sorted(set(s))
        res = ""

        for ch in s:
            if ch in freq:
                freq[ch] += 1
            else:
                freq[ch] = 1

        while freq:
            for ch in letters:
                if ch in freq:
                    if freq[ch] > 0:
                        res += ch
                        freq[ch] -= 1
                    if freq[ch] == 0:
                        del freq[ch]

            for ch in letters[::-1]:
                if ch in freq:
                    if freq[ch] > 0:
                        res += ch
                        freq[ch] -= 1
                    if freq[ch] == 0:
                        del freq[ch]

        return res
